NAVAJO COUNTY BOARD OF SUPERVISORS’ MINUTES 
Tuesday, July 12, 2016 
 
PRESENT: Dawnafe Whitesinger, Supervisor; Robert Black, Supervisor; Steve Williams, Supervisor; Jesse 
Thompson, Vice-Chairman; and Jason Whiting, Chairman.  
9:05 a.m.  Call To Order: Pledge of Allegiance and Invocation were given by Chairman Whiting. 
CALL TO THE PUBLIC: Carrie Hughes, White Mountain Lakes, addressed the Board regarding the issue of 
boat docks in the White Mountain Lakes County Recreation Improvement District. She spoke about one particular 
community member who is wanting a boat dock, and believes that this community member causing a raucous is 
nonsense. She spoke about how Roy and Norma as lake patrol are doing an excellent job.  Jeff Stephenson, White 
Mountain Lakes, stated that he is against having any more boat docks on White Mountain Lakes.  He stated that 
everyone having a boat dock would kill it for the people who are not living around the lake  and encouraged the 
Board to not allow any more boat docks.  Donna Gierst, White Mountain Lakes, addressed the Board indicating 
that other than two days over the holiday weekend, she has never observed more than one boat on the lake at a 
time. She indicated that White Mountain Lake is the quietest lake she has ever been on. She stated for the record 
that private boat docks have always been allowed on the lake. She referred to the Board of Supervisors’ minutes 
in 2005 which set up the fact that homeowners can have the amenity of a private boat dock.  She spoke about 
having audiotape of individuals dismantling boat docks.  She stated that she has petition ed the Advisory 
Committee at White Mountain Lake for a private boat dock and was deliberately deceived by being given the 
2002 Boat Dock Policy . She read the introductory page of the 2001 Rules and Regulations booklet, the sixth 
version. She asked the Board to support the original intent of the Recreation Improvement District to improve the 
recreation on White Mountain Lake. She further asked the Board to task the Advisory Committee to clearly outline 
what their true purpose is and what it should remain.  Eric Gierst, White Mountain Lakes, thanked the Board for 
their concern and oversight for the White Mountain Lake County Recreation Improvement District.  He stated 
that this oversight was necessary to ensure that the rights of the property owners are protected and that the vision 
for the lake is preserved for those property owners.  He spoke about the unique arr angement of White Mountain 
Lake in Navajo County , as well as the Irrigation Dist rict. He stated that since the creation of the Advisory 
Committee, it has been steering away from its name as Advisory Committee of the White Mountain Lake County 
Recreation Improvement District and has become an impediment to its members, many of whom have stated they 
no longer use the lake due to incidents of harassment and confrontation.  He stated that he believes the 
fundamental problem is that the management of White Mountain Lake County Recreation Improvement District 
no longer serves under a stated vision or mission and gave an example.  He also stated that the rules ar e being 
applied arbitrarily and improperly.  He suggested that White Mountain Lake County Recreation Improvement 
District should work under a clearly stated vision with decisions being measured against this vision for 
consistency; second, the White Mountai n Lake County Recreation Improvement District should be consistent 
with the  founding recreation district ; third, the rules should be consistent with its charter and must not be 
arbitrarily applied; and it is up to the committee to put a sane and usable policy in place and make sure they are 
consistent with the stated use of the lake .  Chairman Whiting indicated that the Advisory Committee will hold 
a special meeting on July 20 , 2016, at 6:00 p.m., at which time they will be addressing a new boat dock policy , 
which will then come before this board on July 26, 2016.  
CONSENT AGENDA:  a) Minutes:  June 14 , 2016; June 28, 2016 Executive Session  b) Warrant & Voucher 
Report over $1000 June 2016 c) Justice Court Reports:  Holbrook Precinct #1, June; Winslow Precinct #2, June; 
Snowflake, Precinct #3, June; Kayenta, Precinct #4, June; Show Low Precinct #5, June  2016; and Pinetop -

<!-- page 2 -->
Lakeside, Precinct #6, May 2016 d) Clerk of Superior Court Report June 2016 e) Juvenile Probation Report June 
2016 f) Approval of 20 Back Tax Land Parcels sold over the counter in the amount of $5,443.47 g) Extension of 
Premises/Patio Permit for The Wild Women Saloon and Grill on July 23, 2016, for the Charity Chili Cook-off h) 
Extension of Premises/Patio Permit for The Wild Women Saloon  and Grill on August 6, 2016, for the Crawfish 
Festival i) Grant Agreement HT-16-2640 with City of Tucson for High Intensity Drug Trafficking Areas (HIDTA) 
funding for $12,000 j) Sympathy Letter to Venee John: Supervisor Thompson made a motion to authorize the 
Chairman to sign the items in the consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Whitesinger. Vote unanimous 
approving the motion.  
ADMINISTRATIVE SERVICES:  
a. Presentation to retiring employee Susan Shaw: Eric Scott presented Susan Shaw with a certificate and set of 
Navajo County Coast ers for her retirement after 25 -plus years of service to Navajo County. Mike Espino , 
Deputy County Attorney, spoke about Susan Shaw, her attributes and her service to Navajo County. Ms. Shaw 
spoke of an upcoming kayak trip with her granddaughter.  Supervisor Williams thanked Ms. Shaw for her 
service in Child Support. Chairman Whiting thanked Ms. Shaw for her service.  
b. Consideration and possible approval of Personnel Actions: Eric Scott reviewed the 54-item personnel action 
report. Supervisor Whitesinger made a motion to approve the Personnel Action Report as presented; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Black. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
c. Cedar Fire Recognition: Mary Springer presented a brief slide show prepared by Adam Wolfe recapping the 
Cedar Fire. She stated that she believed that the cause of the fire was human caused.  She gave a recap of the 
events during the Cedar Fire.  She indicated that it was a community effort which led to being able to contain 
the fire and protect the citizens and keep the public informed.  Sheriff KC Clark indicated that he has been 
involved in many fires and believes that, unfortunately, we are getting good at this due to the drought and the 
forest not being thinned properly . Sheriff Clark thanked the members of the Board for their support during 
this fire and those from around the state, including Governor Ducey, Hunter Moore, and the White Mountain 
Apache Tribe. He stated that everybody worked toget her to make sure our citizens were kept safe.  Mary 
Springer advised that many certificates have been distributed to individuals and organizations in recognition 
of their contribution supporting the emergency operations during the Cedar Fire response, incl uding White 
Mountain Apache Tribe; Law Enforcement led by Navajo County Sheriff’s Officers and others from around 
the State ; emergency management from around the State , including Navajo County, Apache County, 
Coconino County, Maricopa County, Gila County ; the Governor’s office, including Hunter Moore ; Jeff 
Whitney, the State Forester ; American Red Cross ; Salvation Army ; Snowflake-Taylor Community 
Emergency Response Team and VIP’s; Snowflake schools; Holbrook schools; Blue Ridge schools; Cibecue 
schools; Eagar schools; the Humane Society of the White Mountains and in the Valley; the Search and Rescue 
volunteers; Facility Management; Public Works; support staff; the Board; the Finance Department, who made 
sure payroll got processed correctly;  the Health Department, including Animal Control, sanitarians, EOCs, 
and Mary Herring, the Director ; Community Values Team members from the Snowflake -Taylor area;  
President Mills and a couple of doctors on staff who are part of the Community Values Team; Department of 
Emergency and Military Affairs, Wendy Smith Reeve and her staff ; Community Counseling Center; private 
businesses; Incident Command; Department of Agriculture; cities and towns; the 311 Call Center in Apache 
County; Arizona Department of Environmental Qualit y; White Mountain Sheriff’s Posse;  the County 
Mounties; and all of the other volunteers.  Mary Springer recognized Bernard Huser, Joshua Smith, Paul Saiz, 
Bill Bess, Ricky Denton, Melissa Buckley, Joyce Alexander, Leann Baker, Shawna Clements, Jennifer Flake, 
Scott Flake, Catrina Jenkins, Jeff Lineberry, Janelle Linn, Robert Schlesinger, Kathy Solomon, Jonnel 
Szentgyorgyi, Don Wells, Adam Wolfe, Wayne Cole, and Gwend ena Lee-Gatewood. Sheriff KC Clark 
recognized the Sheriff’s Auxiliary Volunteers of Cedar  Hills, Sheriff’s Auxiliary Volunteers of White 
Mountain Lakes, Sheriff’s Auxiliary Volunteers of Pinedale and Clay Springs, Sheriff’s Auxiliary Volunteers

<!-- page 3 -->
of High Country, Sheriff’s Auxiliary Volunteers of Heber-Overgaard, White Mountain Sheriff’s Posse, the 
County Mounties, Navajo County Search and Rescue, Chief James Molesa, Deputy Chief Nathan Christensen, 
Deputy Chief Cheryl Powell, Toni Garver, Don Griffiths, Sgt. Wylie Williams, Randy Moffitt, Randy Keith, 
Vince Palazzolo, Dustin Matkin, Connor Francis, Leigh Ann Yazzie, Audriana Wood, Marla Queiruga, Donna 
McMonigal, Mattie Malone, Veronica Rogers, Patricia Gabaldon, and Kya Carroll. Sheriff Clark thanked the 
Board for giving them this opportunity to honor those who were recognized. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked 
Ms. Springer and Sheriff Clark for their work and diligence. She stated that she believes that why we do this 
so well is because people all over the County and those who work for the County love service for their fellow 
man. She thanked every one who received a certificate. Supervisor Thompson thanked everyone involved 
for meeting the challenges of the Cedar fire. He indicated that he was amazed at how things were organized 
and communicated clearly and unanimously. He thanked the first responders. He spoke of the Rodeo-Chediski 
fire. He stated that his biggest concern was for the public and what they were going through.  He thanked our 
team for meeting the challenges. Supervisor Williams spoke about what Chairman Lupe said was that we 
live together and we will fight together. He thank ed the people who live in the affected neighborhoods. He 
told of a story of one of his neighbors reaching out to another neighbor who was elderly to make sure there 
was a plan should they have to evacuate . He shared the saying:  Pray as if everything depends on the Lord 
and work as if everything depends on you.  He indicated that he believed that there was divine intervention in 
protecting the communities and felt the blessings from above. Supervisor Black  echoed the previous 
comments of the Board and thanked staff and all the people who were involved. Chairman Whiting thanked 
all those who were here today and couldn’t be here today. He expressed appreciation for the partnership with 
the White Mountain Apache Tribe.  He spoke of the organization and discipline of all those who helped. He 
expressed appreciation for law enforcement and the manner in which this fire was handled. He thanked all 
law enforcement for working to protect the citizens that they represent. He expressed appreciation to staff for 
all their hard work and Ms. Springer and her leadership. He spoke of the Mayors, Councilmembers, religious 
leaders, the Governor, State leaders, Apache County, Santa Cruz County, Gila County and various counties 
throughout the State. He spoke of all those who called and offered help. He thanked Jason Hatch from APS 
for their support. He stated that the prayers from the community were heard and answered.  Chairman 
Whiting recognized Sheriff KC Clark and Ms. Mary Springer for their leadership with a plaque. Adam Wolfe, 
who was absent, was also recognized with a plaque. Supervisor Thompson stated that he felt very good and 
very proud how members of the Reservation communities have really come together . Catrina Jenkins 
recognized Ms. Springer for all that she has done for the Emergency Management team each and every day  
and presented Ms. Springer with flowers.  
d. Update on Fire Restrictions: Mary Springer gave the Board an update on Fire Restrictions. She s poke about 
this year being different from any other fire season she has witnessed in the County.  She spoke of having a 
major fire in the Pinedale-Clay Springs area in March. She stated that each week the White Mountain Fire 
Restrictions Working Group gets toge ther on a teleconference and discuss es the weather conditions and the 
forecast for the next week. She stated that significant wide-spread moisture is what constitutes when we will 
come out of restrictions and that the forecast from last week for the next ten days is hot, dry and windy. She 
spoke about Highway 277 being closed today due to brush fires caused by a blown tire.  She stated that we 
are in Stage One Restrictions, and there does not seem to be any moisture in sight. Sheriff KC Clark indicated 
that he supports Stage Two Fire Restrictions. Ms. Springer stated that the White Mountain Fire Restrictions 
Working Group meets every week, and tomorrow’s topic will consider where we are at and where we should 
be.  
e. Consideration and possible adoption of Resolution No. 12 -16 Sitgreaves Community and Central Navajo 
County Wildfire Protection Plans for Navajo County : Mary Springer discussed the adoption of Resolution 
Number 12-16 Wildfire Protection Plans. She indicated that this went out for public comment for 30 days and

<!-- page 4 -->
no comment s were received . Supervisor Whitesinger  made a motion to adopt Resolution No. 12 -16 
Sitgreaves Community and Central Navajo County Wildfire Protection Plans for Navajo County ; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Williams. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
f. Consideration and possible award of a contract to Grasso Law Firm to provide legal counsel to our local 
Public Safety Personnel Retirement System (PSPRS) and Corrections Officer Retirement System (CORP) 
Boards: Paige Peterson discussed the item. She indicated that we had two responsive bidders and that she does 
not anticipate that the cost will be more than $5 ,000.00. Supervisor Williams made a motion  to award a 
contract to Grasso Law Firm to provide legal counsel to our local Public Safety Personnel Retirement System 
(PSPRS) and Corrections Officer Retirement System (CORP) Boards; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
SHERIFF:  Consideration and possible approval of Intergovernmental Agreement with White Mountain Apache 
Tribe for cross deputization of NCSO deputies : Sheriff Clark stated that this intergovernmental agreement will 
extend the White Mountain Apache Tribe ’s cross deputization of Navajo County Sheriff’s Office deputies  for 
three more years. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Sheriff Clark, Chief Webster and the White Mountain Apache 
Tribe for helping to establish and continue this partnership . She thanked Brad Carlyon and Gwendena Lee-
Gatewood for their work on this intergovernmental agreement . Supervisor Whitesinger  made a motion to 
approve the Intergovernmental Agreement with White Mountain Apache Tribe for cross deputization of NCSO 
deputies; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson . Vote unanimous approving the motion.  Supervisor 
Thompson thanked Sheriff Clark for his willingness to work with other personnel. Chief Tim Webster, with the 
White Mountain Apache Tribe Police, thanked the Board for their support of this intergovernmental agreement. 
Ms. Lee-Gatewood, on behalf of Ronnie Lupe, expressed appreciation for the opportunity to work with Navajo 
County and is thankful for the teamwork that we have. Supervisor Whitesinger thanked Chief Webster and Ms. 
Lee-Gatewood for being here today to represent the White Mountain Apache Tribe and for their service.  
NAVAJO COUNTY PUBLIC HEALTH SERVICES DISTRICT: Board of Directors Session:  CONSENT 
AGENDA:  Supervisor Williams made a motion to enter into the Navajo County Public Health Services District 
Board of Directors Session; moti on seconded by Supervisor White singer.  Vote unanimous approving the 
motion. 
a. Subcontract between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Coconino County Public Health 
Services District funded through First Things First for Navajo Nation Nutrition  and Obesity Prevention 
Services effective July 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $276,426.00 
b. Subcontract between Navajo County Public Health Services District and Coconino County Public Health 
Services District for the Navajo Nation Oral Health Pr ogram funded through the First things First from July 
1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount of $140,260.00 
c. Professional Services Agreement with Around the Mountain Pediatric Dentistry, PLCC, for dental services 
related to Teledentistry through Arizona First Things First Oral Health Program effective July 1, 2016 to June 
30, 2017 in the amount of $50.00/per hour 
d. Professional Services Agreement with Kenneth F. Rice, DMD, for dental services related to Teledentistry 
through Arizona First Things First Oral Health Program effective July 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount 
of $50.00/per hour 
e. Professional Services Agreement with Joseph D. Tenney, DDS, for dental services related to Teledentistry 
through Arizona First Things First Oral Health Program effective July 1, 2016 to June 30, 2017 in the amount 
of $50.00/per hour Supervisor Williams made a motion to authorize the Chairman to sign the items in the 
consent agenda; motion seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
Supervisor Will iams made a motion to return to regular session; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Whitesinger.  Vote unanimous approving the motion.

<!-- page 5 -->
PUBLIC WORKS:  
a. Consideration and possible award of Contract #B16 -05-018 Pavement Markings to the lowest responsible 
bidder Pavement Marking, Inc.: Glenn Kephart discussed the item. Steve Williams made a motion to award 
Contract #B16-05-018 Pavement Markings to the lowest responsible bidder Pavement Marking, Inc.; motion 
seconded by Supervisor Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion. 
b. Consideration and possible approval of Task Agreement No. 5 to Memorandum of Agreement between 
Bureau of Indian Affairs, Navajo Region and Navajo County for the purchase of equipment parts and supplies 
for an amount not to exceed $25,000.00 from District I CIP Funds:  Pulled for future agenda.  
COUNTY BUSINESS UPDATE:  Report from Board members, County Attorney and County Manager : 
Supervisor Whitesinger spoke of the White Mountain Apache Tribe Annual Youth Conference at which she 
will be a guest speaker.  She thanked the Public Works Office and Mr. Kephart’s leadership in going out to the 
community and doing a site visit and coming up with some solutions . Supervisor Black  expressed his 
appreciation to Mr. Kephart  for coming out  and becoming familiar with the area of his district . Supervisor 
Williams spoke about the Show Low Community Garden ribbon cutting. He thanked Mr. Kephart and the Public 
Works team for meeting with contractors in Heber-Overgaard to discuss partnerships. He spoke about parades in 
Heber-Overgaard, Show Low, and Clay Springs in which he participated . Supervisor Thompson spoke about 
the Red Dog Graveling Project and commended Mr. Kephart and the Public Works team for working diligently 
on this project. He spoke about being invited to speaking engag ements for the youth in Indian Wells. He spoke 
about a running event sponsored by Vice-President Jonathan Nez. He stated that he was invited to Twin Arrows 
for the riding in Siih Hasin kick off.  Chairman Whiting reminded everyone about the White Mountain Facility 
open house today in Show Low. He further reminded everyone about the Wild West activities this weekend in 
Holbrook and stated that he will need to raise $250.00 to keep him out of jail for this celebration. He  stated that 
White Mountain Lakes sent a letter thanking the County for their support, specifically, for the clean -up that was 
done.  He further thanked leadership for acting quickly during the active shooter event that happened two weeks 
ago. He stated that our hearts and prayers are with the families of the victims.  He thanked law enforcement and 
the Court system leadership, including County Attorney’s Office and Judge Ruechel, for speaking with employees 
the next day to express how the County can help them. Supervisor Thompson advised that he traveled with the 
victim rights representative and visited with the family of the victims of the shooting on behalf of the Board 
members. 
 ADJOURN: At 10:34.  Supervisor Willia ms made a motion to adjourn; motion seconded by Supervisor 
Thompson. Vote unanimous approving the motion.  
1:00 P.M. - 5:00 P.M. WHITE MOUNTAIN FACILITY OPEN HOUSE:  Pursuant to ARS § 38-431.02 and 
ARS § 38-431 (3), notice is hereby given to the members of the Navajo County Board of Supervis ors and to the 
General Public that a quorum of the Board of Supervisors may be present for the White Mountain Facility Open 
House at the Navajo County White Mountain Facility, 550 N. 9 th Street, Show Low, Arizona, where no official 
action will be taken. 
 
APPROVED:        DATED:  July 26 , 2016  
 
Jason E. Whiting, Chairman 
Navajo County Board of Supervisors
